# Evidence ledger

(Source prefix F## is the findings-file number. Labels: FACT = reported number or document; MGMT = management claim; OPINION = analyst, expert or agent inference. "calc" marks derived arithmetic.)

E001 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Q2'26 X = 83% of HT Gb but 59% of HT revenue. Implied revenue per Gb: X 0.71, 6000 2.41 (index, HT avg = 1.0), so the 6000 earns ~3.4x the X per Gb and X is ~70% cheaper. Rounding ±1 pt in both shares gives 3.1–3.8x. | OPINION (calc on FACT shares) | F08, F05, F01, F11; ILMN Q2'26 call+deck 2026-07-30 | corroboration: 4
E002 | Q1-OVEREARNING | 6000÷X revenue-per-Gb ratio path: 2.2x (Q2'24), 2.2x (Q3'24), 2.8x (Q4'24–Q2'25), 3.4x (Q3'25), 3.3x (Q4'25), 3.7x (Q1'26), 3.4x (Q2'26). X's own index is stable at 0.60–0.71. The gap widened because the remaining 6000 volume is the high-price tail, not because X repriced. | OPINION (calc) | F08 §2b; F01 | corroboration: 2
E003 | Q1-OVEREARNING | 6000 still earns ~$219M/qtr in Q2'26, ~$0.88B annualized (range $0.83–0.92B). This assumes HT is 69% of sequencing consumables (65–72% sensitivity). Cross-check: FY25 ~1,360 base × ~$670K ≈ $0.9B. | OPINION (calc) | F08; F02 | corroboration: 2
E004 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Converting 100% of remaining 6000 volume at constant Gb and today's relative prices cuts HT consumables ~29% (−$617M/yr, ~−20% of seq. consumables run-rate). Break-even needs +41% total HT Gb, or +240% on the converting Gb alone. | OPINION (calc) | F08, F05, F01 | corroboration: 3
E005 | Q1-OVEREARNING | If ~20% of 6000 volume never moves (Thaysen's rule of thumb), the hit is ~−23% of HT (−$494M/yr, −16% of seq. consumables), and +30% Gb offsets it. | OPINION (calc) | F08, F01 | corroboration: 2
E006 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Each further 5 pts of X volume share costs ~8.5% of HT revenue at constant Gb (~5.9% of seq. consumables, ~$45M/qtr). X at 85% = −$18M/qtr; X at 90% = −$64M/qtr (−11.9% HT). The 2H26 drag from the 80–85% clinical target is only ~$18–35M/qtr. | OPINION (calc) | F08 §3 | corroboration: 1
E007 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Timing matters more than size. Gb +30% and within-platform price −5%: moving X to 90% over 12 months gives HT revenue ~+9%; to 95% gives ~−2%. At Gb +25% and X 90%, ~+5%. Risk is compressed, lumpy conversion, not the endpoint. | OPINION (calc) | F08; F01 | corroboration: 2
E008 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Most H1'26 per-Gb decline is mix, not within-platform repricing. Mix shift (X 69%→83%) explains ~−16.3%. Blended revenue/Gb is −19.5% at 30% Gb growth, leaving a residual of −3.7% (−7% at 35% Gb). Once mix shift ends the headwind should shrink toward this residual, but the 35B cut (~−20%) lands on top. | OPINION (calc) | F08 §2c | corroboration: 1
E009 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Remaining 6000 Gb is overwhelmingly clinical. Backing out from 83% = 78%·c + 92%·(1−c) gives clinical ≈64% of HT Gb (55–65% range), so ~80–83% of remaining 6000 Gb is clinical. If $/Gb is equal across segments, ~$0.7B/yr of 6000 revenue is clinical. | OPINION (calc) | F02, F08 | corroboration: 2
E010 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Clinical X share of volume: ~35% (Q2'24), ~40% (Q3'24), ~50% (Q4'24), >50% (Q1'25), ~55% (Q2'25), ~64% (Q3'25), >2/3 (Q4'25; Freedom says 71%), 76% (Q1'26), 78% (Q2'26). The curve is flattening: only ~11 pts added in two quarters Q4'25→Q2'26. Research 92%. | FACT | F02, F08; ILMN calls 2024-11-04 to 2026-07-30 | corroboration: 3
E011 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Clinical X-volume end-2026 target has drifted: Q4'25 "substantially complete by end of 2026"; Q1'26 Dhingra "over 80% to 85%"; Thaysen to Canaccord "over 85%"; Q2'26 80–85%. Even the best case leaves 15–20% of clinical volume on the 6000. | MGMT | F02, F08; ILMN Q4'25/Q1'26/Q2'26 calls | corroboration: 2
E012 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Thaysen (Feb 2025): ~20% of Gb "stay and shrink over time; the last portion takes much longer than the first 75–80%." This is the clearest management admission of a sticky 6000 tail. | MGMT | F01; ILMN Q4'24 call 2025-02-06 | corroboration: 2
E013 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Dhingra (Nov 2024): some customers will, in his personal belief, never move existing tests off the 6000. | MGMT | F01; Wolfe 2024-11-20 | corroboration: 1
E014 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Q2'26 call, "over-earning" question (Tycho Peterson, Jefferies per F02/F16; F14 could not confirm the firm). Thaysen: many 6000s still in action, "customers actually purchasing a few 6000s this quarter", HiSeq "in the portfolio until a few quarters ago", no "true headwind". No 6000 revenue, decommission pace or sizing given. Dhingra did not answer. | MGMT/FACT | F02, F07, F14, F16; ILMN Q2'26 call 2026-07-30 | corroboration: 4
E015 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Illumina sold ">10" new 6000s in Q2'26 (customers "plan to stay on it for years") vs ~30 shipped in all of FY25. | FACT | F02, F07, F05, F14; Q2'26 call | corroboration: 4
E016 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Thaysen's framing of the 6000 tail changed: Q2'25 "no cliff", stay until assay end-of-life (~2 yrs); Q1'26 "laggards" with FDA/regulated assays, "long tail" after end-2026; Q2'26 "standard part of the business", "not a cliff, a wave." | MGMT | F02, F07; Q2'25–Q2'26 calls | corroboration: 2
E017 | Q1-OVEREARNING | 6000 pull-through: FY23 guide $800–900K (from ~$1M); FY24 guide $700–800K (net base); FY25 actual ~$670K; FY26 guide $600–650K. Implied Q2'26 ≈$670–680K on an assumed ~1,300 base, i.e. above the FY26 guide. Guggenheim (Q4'25): "holding up better than expected." | FACT/OPINION | F01, F02, F08; Q4'25 deck slide 15 | corroboration: 3
E018 | Q1-OVEREARNING | 6000 pull-through was re-based on a "net" installed base from Feb 2024 (denominator shrinks, metric rises). CFO: pull-through is "simply quarterly consumables divided by the active base"; this "does not change reported revenue." | FACT/MGMT | F01, F07; Q4'23 call 2024-02-08 | corroboration: 2
E019 | Q1-OVEREARNING | 6000 installed base ~1,770 net (end-2023) → ~1,700 (Q3'24) → ~1,360 (end-FY25) after ~330 FY25 decommissions and ~30 shipments. Q1'23 guidance had been "only 10–12 decommissions" among early X buyers; ~110 deactivated by end-2023. | FACT | F01, F02, F07, F08 | corroboration: 4
E020 | Q1-OVEREARNING | No 6000 installed base after Q4'25, no FY26 decommission guide, no count of dual-running customers and no 6000 revenue or Gb is disclosed. Q2'26 X base is also undisclosed (~1,065 calc). | FACT (absence) | F02, F08 | corroboration: 2
E021 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Parallel running evidence: JPM survey (Jun 2026) finds ~75% of clinical respondents still own a 6000 and ~58% an X. The summary page says 16% for the 6000, which conflicts with the chart text. | FACT (survey) | F05; JPM survey 2026-06-10 | corroboration: 1
E022 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Tempus Molecular Technologist posting (Chicago, "6+ months ago") lists "Illumina Novaseq6000 and NovaX" together. Natera RA2 posting loads "NovaSeq and NovaSeq X Plus". Anecdotal. | FACT (posting) | F17; jobs.lightbank.com/companies/tempus/jobs/52994688 | corroboration: 1
E023 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Jan 2026: the largest clinical cohort "either transitions fully to the X or layers incremental volume onto X alongside existing NovaSeq 6000 workflows." Management says 6000 pull-through "stays up" for validated assays. | MGMT | F02; JPM 2026-01-13; Q4'25 call | corroboration: 2
E024 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Placements are mostly incremental: Q1'26 clinical ">60% of placements, mostly incremental rather than conversions of 6000s"; Q2'26 ~70% clinical / 30% research; JPM says up to half of clinical X placements are incremental. | MGMT | F02, F05, F08; Q1'26/Q2'26 calls | corroboration: 3
E025 | Q1-OVEREARNING | 40-customer cohort (Q3'25 deck slide 9): labels only (+DD, +DD, +LSD, +DD), no axis values. Footnote says "fully or largely" transitioned, "including a subset of top-tier accounts"; the call said "fully". Excludes brand-new HT customers. Not updated since 9/28/2025. | FACT | F02, F07; Q3'25 deck | corroboration: 3
E026 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Dhingra: "volume offset price in year 1 and then revenue and volume both accelerated in year 2." Schenkel read year-1 growth as <5% and Thaysen accepted it; Thaysen (JPM Jan 2026): year 1 "a little bit flattish", "almost in an equilibrium". | MGMT | F02, F07; Q3'25 call; Wolfe 2025-11-19 | corroboration: 3
E027 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Cohort selection bias: early converters are the most motivated to expand. Laggards (validated, FDA-approved, price-motivated, low-volume) are absent. Illumina said it will not keep reporting cohorts (JPM Jan 2026). | OPINION | F02, F07 | corroboration: 2
E028 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Thaysen's cohort taxonomy (Wolfe, Nov 2025): (1) never-convert, low volume; (2) old assays stay on 6000 and new larger ones go to X; (3) exome→genome movers; (4) price-motivated movers (e.g. NIPT, "<20%", mostly moved). Cohorts 2 and 3 are "absolutely the largest" and use 25B; cohorts 1 and 4 stay on 10B, "more like high-volume 6000 pricing." | MGMT | F02; Wolfe 2025-11-19 | corroboration: 1
E029 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Bernstein (May 2025): Thaysen said the X cut price "by two-thirds" and volume must rise "100%" to make up revenue. A two-thirds cut needs ~+200% (3x); 100% covers only a one-half cut. | FACT (quote) + calc | F02; Bernstein 2025-05-28 | corroboration: 1
E030 | Q1-OVEREARNING | CFO Aug 2024: 6000→X "overall, all-inclusive, easily more than 60% to 70% price decline on a Gb-to-Gb basis." Observed blended gap was only 54–55% in 2024 and 65–71% from Q3'25. F02 did not find the exact phrase in its files, but F01/F08 locate it in the Q2'24 call. | MGMT | F01, F08; Q2'24 call 2024-08-06 | corroboration: 3
E031 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Revalidation mechanics: Goswami (Nov 2023) said assays validated on the 6K "will not immediately switch"; the X gets "newer assays that were marginally viable or not viable on the 6K." Thaysen (Q3'24): sometimes "validation cost outweighs the savings of moving an existing assay", and the move takes 12–18 months. | MGMT | F01; Evercore 2023-11-28; Q3'24 call | corroboration: 2
E032 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Goswami (Sept/Nov 2023): customers "never batch samples at the rate needed to fill a flow cell… pay a slightly higher effective price that we recover." Never quantified. This is a premium that shrinks as fill rates improve. | MGMT | F01, F08; MS 2023-09-11; Q3'23 call | corroboration: 2
E033 | Q1-OVEREARNING | List price increases applied alongside the transition: Q1'24 "adjust pricing across the portfolio" and annual increases with "very good realization"; Q1'25 tariff price rise on new orders (~1 pt of growth). These likely lift 6000 $/Gb while its base shrinks. | MGMT/OPINION | F01, F02; Q1'24 call; RBC 2024-05-14; Q1'25 call | corroboration: 2
E034 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Core non-GAAP gross margin: Q1'23 65.2, Q2'23 67.0, Q3'23 66.0, Q4'23 64.7, Q1'24 67.1, Q2'24 69.4, Q3'24 70.5, Q4'24 67.4; Q4'25 67.0; Q1'26 68.2; Q2'26 68.2 (vs 69.4 in Q2'25). GM fell as instruments mix, tariffs, memory and freight rose. Consumables margin cannot be isolated, so no visible over-earning in GM. | FACT | F01, F03, F02 | corroboration: 3
E035 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Goswami (Q3'23): 6000 consumables are "highly profitable" and losing them hurts margin. X consumables were "designed so margins at scale would be comparable to NovaSeq 6000", lower early on. No 6000-vs-X margin per Gb disclosed anywhere. | MGMT | F01, F08; Q3'23 and Q4'23 calls | corroboration: 2
E036 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Filings dropped the phrase that other HT consumables decrease "as customers transition to NovaSeq X" (present in FY24 10-K and Q1'25 10-Q; gone from FY25 10-K and Q3'25–Q2'26 10-Qs). "Reduced pricing" also vanished from the gross-margin discussion after Q1'25. | FACT | F03; Sources/ILMN/filing | corroboration: 1
E037 | Q1-OVEREARNING | FY25 10-K inventory reserve is a Critical Audit Matter, with "product transitions (customer migration to NovaSeq X)" a driver. Inventory rose $564M→$655M while the reserve fell $133M→$123M (YE25→Q2'26). | FACT | F03, F07 | corroboration: 2
E038 | Q1-OVEREARNING | X pull-through: FY24 ~$1.3M, FY25 ~$1.35M, FY26 guide $1.35–1.40M. Implied (calc) $1.34–1.37M in Q4'24 and Q4'25 but only $1.19–1.24M in Q1–Q2'26, because new units ramp over 6–9 months (was "roughly six months" in Q1'26). Mgmt: clinical customers need "at least 6 to 9 months" to normalize. | FACT/MGMT | F02, F08, F07 | corroboration: 3
E039 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Reagent rental/lease: ~5 of 100+ placements in Q4'25; some of ~83 Q1'26 units on rental/lease "with different revenue recognition". Q1'26 instrument revenue $118M looked light vs 80+ placements. ~21,000 "active instruments" include rental units. Rental embeds instrument cost recovery in consumable price and may inflate pull-through. Count and economics undisclosed. | FACT/OPINION | F02, F07, F05; Q1'26 call | corroboration: 3
E040 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Derived platform dollars ($M; HT = 69% of seq. consumables): X 136 (Q2'24), 184, 193, 206, 225, 263, 287, 276, 316 (Q2'26). 6000: 367, 327, 289, 274, 286, 252, 234, 225, 219. HT total stays in a ~$480–535M band, so X growth has mostly replaced 6000 dollars. 6000 y/y decline slowed to −$7M in Q2'26 after −$67M in Q2'25. | OPINION (calc) | F08 §2a | corroboration: 1
E041 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Hypothesis from F02: the 6000's pull-through is internally consistent with the reported 41% share (1,360×$670K ≈ $0.9B vs X 890×$1.35M ≈ $1.2B, so the 6000 is 43%). Disclosed pull-through arithmetic is consistent with reported shares. | OPINION (calc) | F02, F08 | corroboration: 2
E042 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Shelf life: X consumables are committed at 3–4 months vs a 6-month minimum for the 6000. X revenue is therefore more sensitive to year-end destocking. | MGMT | F01; Evercore 2024-12-03 | corroboration: 1
E043 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Guardant moved Guardant360 Liquid to X in May 2026, cutting cost per test by ~$200. This is a live case of a clinical customer capturing the X saving. | MGMT | F09, F12; Guardant Q2'26 call 2026-07-30 | corroboration: 2
E044 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Adaptive MRD sequencing gross margin 72% in Q2'26 vs 64% a year earlier, "helped by the NovaSeq X transition." clonoSEQ volume +43% to 36,111. E045 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Under-filled-flow-cell premium, large-deal discounts and list increases are not separable from the data. E046 | PRICING | Quarterly shares X % HT Gb / % HT revenue: Q2'24 ~45/27; Q3'24 55/35–36; Q4'24 65–66/40; Q1'25 68/43; Q2'25 69/44; Q3'25 78/51; Q4'25 ~80/55; Q1'26 82/55; Q2'26 83/59. Q4'25 deck chart appears to show ~90% (possibly research-only). | FACT | F02, F08, F07 | corroboration: 3
E047 | PRICING | Management's own long-term model: volume mid-20s %, price mid-teens % per year, giving HSD revenue. Strategy Day: 25% volume vs 15% price implies "mid-to-high single digit" HT consumables growth. Earlier (Aug 2024) price impact was modeled 20–25%/yr and Q3'24 came in "closer to 25 rather than 20." | MGMT | F01, F02, F07; Strategy Day 2024-08-13; JPM 2026-01-13 | corroboration: 3
E048 | PRICING | Q3'24: Gb +40% vs consumables +5–7%, "largely price… about, say, 25% decline on the price" (Dhingra). Q2'24: Gb +40% vs HT revenue ~+1%. | FACT/MGMT | F01; Wolfe 2024-11-20; Q2'24 and Q3'24 calls | corroboration: 2
E049 | PRICING | Gb growth reported: Q2'23 +9% (run-based), Q3'23 +29%, Q4'23 +46%, Q1'24 >35%, Q2'24 >40%, Q3'24 >40%, Q4'24 onward ">30%" (a floor, hiding the true gap). Clinical "well above" 30%. The 10-yr average is ~25%. | FACT | F01, F08, F02 | corroboration: 3
E050 | PRICING | Sequencing consumables $M: Q1'25 696, Q2'25 740, Q3'25 747, Q4'25 755, Q1'26 726 (+4.3%), Q2'26 775 (+4.7%); FY23 2,790; FY24 2,858 (Bloomberg) or 2,872 (deck); FY25 2,939. Includes labor, overhead and shipping; internal rates run 35–50% below external. | OPINION (calc on FACT lists) | F11; Salk, UConn, Calgary, CWRU, Dana-Farber price lists | corroboration: 1
E052 | PRICING | At Salk, a 25B-300 flow cell ($42,560) costs 7% more than an S4-300 ($39,701) for ~2.7x the Gb. The per-Gb decline is a volume effect, so customers must grow Gb to hold spend. | FACT (calc) | F11; salk.edu core rates 07/01/25 | corroboration: 1
E053 | PRICING | The 6000 is itself repricing: Salk S4-300 $52,098 (older, undated list) → $39,701 (07/01/25), −24%. S4-200 $43,586 → $36,708, −16%. Dartmouth FY27 internal S4 at $3.91/Gb is an outlier. | FACT | F11 | corroboration: 1
E054 | PRICING | 25B core prices ~$2.5–3.3/Gb on cheaper lists: CWRU Jul 2026 $2.63, Dana-Farber $2.50–2.88, UConn 2026 $2.94, TxGen 2024 $3.11, TGen Aug 2026 $3.14. Illumina's own list is $2.00/Gb. The 5B flow cell (~$3.20/Gb at CWRU) appeared Jul 2026. | FACT | F11 | corroboration: 1
E055 | PRICING | Illumina's launch claim: X is "up to 59%" lower cost/Gb than S4 at $2.00/Gb list (~$200 per 100 Gb genome, consumables only). | MGMT | F11; Illumina X post (2022) / illumina.com | corroboration: 1
E056 | PRICING | Late 2025: Illumina "harmonised" cost per read across 5B/10B/25B flow cells, buying in 600M read-pair units (5B lane=1, 10B=2, 25B=5). No figures disclosed. | MGMT (via NGI Sweden) | F11; ngisweden.scilifelab.se/?p=11245, 2025-11-17 | corroboration: 1

E187 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Clinical-only recalculation. Assumes the same 6000-vs-X $/Gb ratio in clinical and research, which no source confirms. Total-HT data imply X $/Gb is about 0.295x the 6000's (~70.5% cheaper). Clinical is ~64% of HT Gb (57-71% range). Clinical X revenue share is ~51% (research ~77%). Clinical 6000 revenue is ~$182M/qtr (range $172-191M, ~$0.73B/yr). That is ~36% of clinical sequencing consumables (~$504M/qtr) while the 6000 holds only ~22% of clinical HT Gb. Clinical HT revenue is ~$372M/qtr. Clinical break-even Gb growth if all remaining clinical 6000 Gb converts: +53% at the observed ratio, +42% at a 60% cut, +52% at a 70% cut. Total-HT break-even is +41% (E004). At +30% Gb and a 70% cut, clinical HT revenue falls ~15% (~$54M/qtr). If Gb growth applies only to the converted Gb, break-even is +150% (60% cut) or +233% (70% cut). Consistency check: the same method at Q3'25 gives clinical X revenue ~34%, in line with Wolfe's 'less than 50%' (E104). | OPINION (calc) | F21; Q2'26 deck slide 8, 2026-07-30 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 21, 22, 23, 24)
E188 | Q1-OVEREARNING | The only quantification of 6000 revenue loss in Illumina's filings is FY2023. Lower NovaSeq 6000 consumables pull-through cut sequencing consumables revenue by $92M (Q1'23), $5M (Q2'23) and $30M (Q3'23), $127M for FY23, 'driven primarily by lower NovaSeq 6000 consumables pull-through'. The FY25 10-K and 2026 10-Qs do not name the 6000 in the revenue discussion. | FACT | F21; ILMN 10-Q Q1/Q2/Q3 2023 and 10-K FY2023 (Sources/ILMN/filing), 2023-2024 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 21, 22, 23, 24)

CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E015 : The '>10 NovaSeq 6000' placement line was Dhingra's, in CFO prepared remarks ('as some consumers [sic] plan to remain on that platform for years to come'), not Thaysen's and not Q&A (call l.156, F21)

X is 58% of all respondents (36/62), 53% of clinical (17/32, not 58%), 63% of academic. The '16%' is a typo. 75% means 'owns at least one', not volume share, and the survey is silent on dual running (JPM 2026-06-15 p.8, F21)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E067 : The 4-5% implied clinical price decline is JPM's inference from stated forward plans (spend-growth bucket answers vs separately asked volume, n=32 clinical). It is not realized pricing and is noisy (JPM survey p.11, F21)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E059 : The UBS expert note is dated 26 Feb 2026 (not just 'Feb 2026'). It is one 'Doc Debates' expert, not a clinical customer. He expected Roche at $100/genome and said the 35B 'likely brings Illumina pricing to $150 anyway' (F21)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E060 : $0.06 vs $0.68 per M reads is confirmed (UBS First Read 2026-03-03). It is Roche's quote vs Illumina's list price on the 25B flow cell, ~11x. The extract garbling is resolved, but it remains not like-for-like (simplex vs list) (F21)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E182 : $689K is the Element VITARI list price, not an X Plus price. UBS says that is ~40% below NovaSeq X+, implying ~$1.15M, consistent with Vilella's $1.165M (UBS Fast Take 2026-02-19, F21)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E069 : Add VITARI consumables of ~$3k vs ~$32k per run and output of 3T vs 16T, so ~$1.00/Gb vs ~$2.00/Gb (UBS 2026-02-19, F21)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E004 : Total-HT break-even of +41% understates the clinical requirement. Clinical break-even is +53% at the observed 70.5% price ratio, or +42% to +52% at 60-70% cuts, because clinical has ~22% of its Gb still on the 6000 vs 17% for total HT (F21, calc)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E003/E009 : Clinical is ~83% of 6000 revenue, ~$182M/qtr (range $172-191M, ~$0.73B/yr), equal to ~36% of clinical sequencing consumables. This tightens the 'clinical ≈ $0.7B' estimate (F21, calc)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E104 : The deck-based method gives clinical X revenue ~34% at Q3'25 and ~51% at Q2'26, consistent with Wolfe's 'less than 50%' in Nov 2025 (F21)
CORRECTION (21, 22, 23, 24): E150/E151 : Meister's sales total ~3.02M shares and ~$586M across six Form 4s, which supports the $591M figure. The ~$430M figure is only the Aug-Sep leg (~$431M). CORRECTION (33, 34, 35, 36): E152 / CORRECTION on E152 (Thaysen holdings): the 107,770 figure is the correct direct balance after a Code F tax-withholding of 1,303 shares at $293.69 on 2026-10-05 (Form 4 accession 0001110803-26-000183), not an error. The 83,390 figure is also right for May 2025 and the later prints in the ledger (82,089; 89,883; 83,339) are earlier-dated balances. Only the '100 shares' purchase version stays doubtful. Code F is withholding (~$383K), not a 10b5-1 sale. Earlier summarizer labels of Code F as '10b5-1 sales' may be unreliable (SEC Form 4 XML).
CORRECTION (33, 34, 35, 36): E197 / E149 / CORRECTION 255 (PSU range): the 0–250% range is confirmed for both PSU tranches in the Aug–Sep 2026 grants to the new CCO Sullivan (5,254 target shares per tranche, accession 0001110803-26-000169) and CLO Coletti (5,307 per tranche, 0001110803-26-000177). The two tranches are three-year EPS growth and relative TSR, vesting 2028-12-31. No 200% figure was found in the 2026 grants. The Thaysen 2025 PSU footnote (search extract) reads 'three-year average consolidated non-GAAP EPS growth FY2026–2028… 0% to 250%'. That contradicts E197's 'operating margin plus rTSR, no EPS PSUs' and supports the 10-Q wording that EPS PSUs resumed. The metric is unverified until the CD&A is read, and the FY2026–28 window on a March 2025 grant is odd. The 2025 grant-date values visible in the extract (PSUs $3,433,033 and $2,870,015; RSUs $2,460,074) total only ~$8.76M, not the $12.3M in E255.
CORRECTION (33, 34, 35, 36): E197 : the AIP target for the CEO was raised from 125% to 150% of salary in FY25, so the 77% payout ($1,155,000) is on a higher target than the prior year's (DEF 14A search extract, 0001193125-26-149669). Thaysen's $11,018,895 SCT total is now confirmed from XBRL.

FY25 10-K commitments note (R16, as of 31 Dec 2025) totals $256.9M: laboratory-instruments supplier $18.9M (2026 $9.2M, 2027 $9.7M), material suppliers $107.3M (2026 $89.3M, 2027 $7.3M, 2028 $8.2M, 2029 $2.6M), application service providers $13.6M, cloud $28.0M, other $89.1M. The note does not name Illumina. Material-supplier commitments are about 13% of FY25 product cost of revenue (~$811M, R20) and fall to $7M in 2027, so long-dated Illumina volume is not locked in. The Eleventh Amendment (eff. 2 Mar 2026) keeps a quarterly minimum with the amount redacted (that detail is from a search summary of the exhibit). E228 | CUSTOMERS | Guardant's Illumina contract, read in the primary exhibit (Amendment #5, signed 30 Dec 2020), has no minimum purchase obligation, no exclusivity and no tariff clause. E231 | COMPETITORS | Illumina's published benchmark of the Ultima UG 100 against the NovaSeq X on whole-genome data: the UG 100 shows '6x more SNV errors and 22x more indel errors' against the full NIST v4.2.1 benchmark. Ultima's high-confidence region excludes ~4.2% of the genome, 2.3% of the exome, 1.0% of ClinVar variants and ~450,000 variants. Illumina cites X accuracy of SNV 99.94%, CNV 97%, SV 88% and STR 95.2%. This is a vendor claim from a competitor and is untested by a third party. It quantifies the accuracy and region-exclusion hurdle a clinical lab would face moving regulated assays to Ultima (see E116, E129). | MGMT | Illumina, 'Ultima UG 100 vs NovaSeq X WGS data', 2025-10-07, https://www.illumina.com/science/genomics-research/articles/ultima-ug-100-vs-illumina-novaseq-x-wgs-data.html | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 37, 38, 39, 40)
CORRECTION (37, 38, 39, 40): E194 / CORRECTION 242 : Resolved. The Eleventh Amendment §7(g) tariff pass-through is NON-retroactive. Illumina invoices Natera only above a redacted tariff rate. Pass-through starts 30 days after Illumina begins paying the tariff above that rate 'on a non-retroactive basis'. The tariff is the Section 122 duty (Proclamation 11012, 91 Fed. Reg. 9339, 25 Feb 2026) plus any successor Section 301/232 duties. The rate at 2 Mar 2026 was 10%. Natera gets a pro-rata share of later refunds. The 'retroactive' language belongs to Part E, the WGS-MRD price schedule, which is retroactive and tiered. Change E194 to non-retroactive. (Natera 10-Q Q1'26 Ex. 10.1, accession 0001628280-26-032478; WebFetch reading of the exhibit, threshold redacted)
CORRECTION (37, 38, 39, 40): E192 : The keyword search of the Eleventh Amendment text found no 'most favored', 'MFN' or 'lowest price' language. It amends and restates §1, §7(a), §7(f), §7(k) and §7(l) and replaces the 'Volume-Based Net Price' definition. Whether the Eighth Amendment MFN survives, or was dropped when that definition was replaced, is unverified. Treat the MFN as possibly superseded, not as current. (Natera 10-Q Q1'26 Ex. 10.1)
CORRECTION (37, 38, 39, 40): E212 : §7(l) is a ceiling and not a floor. It caps the inflation-adjusted $/Gb on new product versions at the prior Net Price, so no clause protects Illumina's revenue per Gb. This supports the existing E212 reading. The gap 1 question of whether §7(l) is a 'contractual $/Gb floor' is answered: it is not. (Natera Q1'26 Ex. 10.1)
CORRECTION (37, 38, 39, 40): E054/E055 / CORRECTION 254 : Soften to 'unresolved'. The $2.90/Gb (200-cycle) and $4.68/Gb (100-cycle) figures rest on output scaled by cycle count, which is an assumption, and the Illumina order page shows the same 8-10.5 Tb for every cycle count. Case Western's July 2026 core resale prices for 25B are $15,900 (100-cycle) and $21,000 (300-cycle). Against ~8,000 Gb the 300-cycle price is ~$2.63/Gb, already in E054. The 300-cycle price is only ~1.3x the 100-cycle price, so price is clearly sub-linear in cycles. Illumina's own 300-cycle list price is still not found. (case.edu/medicine/genomics/pricing, fetched 2026-10-11; core resale price, not list)
CORRECTION (37, 38, 39, 40): E109 : The August 2033 Natera term is not in the Eleventh Amendment text. It presumably comes from the 10-K narrative, which has not been read. Guardant's term runs to ~Jan 2033 and Caris's August 2033 date is unverified, so keep the dates separate. (F37)
E232 | CUSTOMERS | The Illumina–Caris Supply Agreement runs '12 years from the closing of the Transaction' (the GRAIL closing, Aug 2021), so it ends Aug 2033. It is the GRAIL open-offer template, which explains why Natera and Caris share an end date. Terms: Caris may terminate at any time without liability on 90 days' notice. The agreement is 'not contingent on any purchase commitments', so there is no take-or-pay, and the IVD kit agreement is non-exclusive. Caris picks Grandfathered or Universal Pricing (tiered volume-based net price). That price must be no less favorable than what GRAIL pays (MFN), and its inflation-adjusted level 'shall not increase' except for material cost increases outside Illumina's control. The Natera and Caris 10-K wording rests on search summaries, so it may differ slightly. Natera's Aug 2033 date is therefore not a conflation. Customers can walk with no volume lock-in, and contractual $/Gb can only fall. | FACT (contract text) / OPINION (implication) | Caris S-1 Ex. 10.2(A), acc. 0001104659-25-052381, 2025, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/2019410/000110465925052381/tm2415719d15_ex10-2a.htm; GRAIL Form 10 Ex. 99.1 (open-offer scope) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 41, 42, 43, 44)

E235 | PRICING | Implied $/Gb on the GSA prices: 25B-300 is $3.02 (at 8 Tb) down to $2.30 (at 10.5 Tb), so it never reaches the $2.00/Gb launch claim even at top output. 10B-300 is $3.62–4.83. 1.5B-300 is $5.49–7.85, about the same per Gb as an S4. E236 | Q1-OVEREARNING | TSO 500 HT (a clinical oncology assay) samples per run at 3,500x coverage: 6000 S2 72; S4 192; X 1.5B 32; X 10B 192; X 25B 480 (up to 960 on an X Plus with two flow cells); turnaround 4–5 days. On GSA kit prices, sequencing cost per sample is about $113 (S4), $75 (X 10B, −33%), $50 (X 25B, −55%) and $217 (S2). So a lab moving S4 to 25B cuts Illumina's consumable revenue per sample about 55%. Samples must rise about 2.2x (25B) or 1.5x (10B) for revenue neutrality. Because 10B and S4 both run 192 samples, the 10B saving is pure price. A 55% cut saves a lab only about $63 per sample against sample prep and informatics of roughly $600–1,100 (E058), and the lowest cost needs a batch of 480. This is the first per-sample economics for a named clinical assay. | FACT (samples/run) / OPINION (calc on GSA prices) | illumina.com TSO 500 HT page, https://www.illumina.com/products/by-type/clinical-research-products/trusight-oncology-500-ht.html, fetched 2026-10-11; M-GL-02869 PDF not read | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 41, 42, 43, 44)

E245 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Guided FY26 pull-through does not fit consensus. X at $1.35-1.40M on an average base of about 1,060 gives about $1.43-1.49B. The 6000 at $600-650K on an average base of about 1,200-1,300 gives about $0.72-0.83B for the year. Since H1'26 6000 revenue is about $444M (225+219), H2 implies $138-200M a quarter (centre about $175M) against $219M in Q2'26. If the 6000 held at $219M, X pull-through would be only about $1.18M, below guide. Guidance and Q3/Q4 consensus ($789M/$815M) are coherent only if the 6000 loses about $40M a quarter (about $175M annualized, about 5.7% of FY26 consumables) within six months. The pre-announced 'not a cliff' therefore already appears in the numbers. The end-2026 bases are the agent's extrapolation and the 69% HT share is an assumption. Q3'26 results on 29 Oct are the test. | OPINION (calc on FACT guides and ledger inputs) | F45 internal bridge, finding 2-3; ILMN Q4'25 deck slide 15 and Q2'26 call, 2026-10-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 45, 46, 47, 48)
E246 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Revenue-at-risk grid with a phasing assumption. Formula: at-risk = $876M annualized 6000 revenue x f x [1-(1-p)(1+u)], with f the share converted, p the price cut and u the volume uplift on converted Gb. Central case (60% cut, +15% uplift): $473M, or 15.2% of FY26 consensus consumables ($3,104M), at 100% conversion. Assuming 25%/60%/80% of the remaining 6000 revenue converts by end-2026/27/28, the central case costs 3.8%/9.1%/12.2% of FY26 consumables. Range at 100% conversion: $307M (9.9%) to $613M (19.8%). Volume uplift is worth only about 2 pts of FY26 consumables per +15%, because the price cut dominates. The phasing is an assumption, not data. | OPINION (calc, assumption-driven) | F45 section B, 2026-10-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 45, 46, 47, 48)
E247 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Price-bridge cross-check against management's mid-teens annual price decline. Three layers add to about the mid-teens model: tail conversion (about 6-7% a year of seq. consumables), the 35B (about 4% a year) and residual within-platform repricing (about 4% a year, from the E008 residual). Management's mid-teens therefore leaves no room for a Roche or Ultima price response. The 35B at constant Gb on an X base of about $1.27B costs $63M/$127M/$253M (2.0%/4.1%/8.2% of FY26 consumables) at 25%/50%/100% adoption. Combined worst-but-plausible by end-2028 (80% conversion, 70% cut, +15% uplift, 60% 35B adoption): about $640M (20.6% of FY26 consumables, about 30% of HT). Break-even is about +26% Gb a year, which sits between E083's +23% and E205's +35-60%. | OPINION (calc) | F45 findings 8-10 and section F, 2026-10-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 45, 46, 47, 48)
E248 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Parallel-run bound from the JPM survey by pigeonhole. Of 32 clinical respondents, 24 own a 6000 and 17 own an X, so at least 9 and at most 17 own both. That is 37.5-71% of clinical 6000 owners. Applied to about $728M of clinical 6000 revenue, $277-517M sits at dual-owner labs. Moving it all to existing X capacity would cost $195-364M a year (6.3-11.7% of FY26 consumables) with no new placements. A 10/20/30% fall in 6000 utilization into idle X capacity costs $62M/$124M/$185M. The count is of customers, not revenue, and n=32. | OPINION (calc on FACT survey counts) | F45 finding 6 and section D; JPM survey 2026-06-15 p.8 (per ledger corrections) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 45, 46, 47, 48)
E249 | CUSTOMERS | The Open Offer template Natera and Caris sit on contains a contractual per-Gb price commitment. Illumina's test-terms page also promises no price increases on covered sequencing products. Combined with the 'shall not increase' clause (E232), this means a contractual price path that can only fall. It helps explain why Gb growth of 40% came with revenue growth of about 5%. Nothing shows the 43% was measured or met. | FACT (contract text and Illumina web page) / OPINION (inference) | Caris S-1 Ex. 10.2(a), sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/2019410/000110465925052381/tm2415719d15_ex10-2a.htm; tst-web.illumina.com/areas-of-interest/cancer/test-terms.html, 2025-2026 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 45, 46, 47, 48)

CORRECTION (45, 46, 47, 48): E210/E041 : The pull-through cross-check should use average bases, since pull-through is quarterly consumables divided by the active base (E018). X is about 760 average x $1.35M = about $1.03B, not about $1.2B on the year-end base (derived dollars $0.98B). 6000 is about 1,510 x $670K = about $1.01B (derived $1.05B). Total is $2.04B against $2.03B (69% x $2,939M), which confirms the 69% HT share to about 0.5%. The earlier 'about 4% overshoot' was partly coincidence, and the 6000 is about 50% of HT dollars on this basis, not 43% (F45 finding 1, calc)
CORRECTION (45, 46, 47, 48): Open gap 4 / Q2'26 10-Q Note 8 : Note 8 (XBRL R14, acc. 0001110803-26-000160) covers GRAIL shareholder litigation only: derivative suits, one settled with no payment, securities class actions with the state class certified in May 2026, and two shareholder demands deferred by the board in Feb 2026. A summarizer found no Element, EC-fine or SEC-investigation text and no dollar accrual. Element and the SEC investigation must be in Item 1A or Part II, so the premise that Note 8 would cover them was wrong. This is paraphrased, not verbatim (F46)
CORRECTION (45, 46, 47, 48): E126 : Broad Clinical Labs' amplification-free AXELIOS workflow needs 500 ng of DNA, so the '150 ng' caveat understates the input requirement for that route. Centogene's results are labelled research (Roche ESHG satellite page, F48)
CORRECTION (45, 46, 47, 48): E077/E211 : The Q2'26 10-Q Note 10 (R16) reports one segment with no clinical or region disaggregation, so 'clinical' exists only in earnings materials and calls, not in audited statements. This corroborates E211 (F46, summarizer reading)

E256 | CUSTOMERS | The Caris open-offer pricing clause carries a refund mechanism: 'Illumina will refund to Customer the difference between the pricing made available to Customer and the triggering pricing, multiplied by the number of affected units'. Total Gb +20% requires the 6000 at +16% $/Gb and the X at −2.6%. +30% requires 6000 +7% and X −10%. +35% requires 6000 +3% and X −13.5%. +40% requires 6000 −0.3% and X −16.5%. +45% requires 6000 −3.7% and X −19.4%. Because the 6000's own lists fell 16–24% (E053), a 6000 price rise of 7% or more is implausible. That points to total Gb of about +33–45%. H2 X revenue must be $844–898M against $592M in H1, so H2 annualized pull-through must be about $1.46–1.55M. That compares with about $1.21M in H1'26 and a ~$1.35–1.37M peak in Q4'25. Q2 ended 28 Jun, so Q3 ends 27 Sep (13 weeks) and Q4 ends 3 Jan 2027 (14 weeks). A 14th week is mechanically 1/13 = 7.7% of Q4 consumables, or about 2% of FY revenue. E270 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Mapping for the Q3'26 X revenue-share print. v = s·r/(s·r+1−s), with r the X/6000 $/Gb ratio. At r=0.28–0.31, an X volume share of 85% gives a revenue share of 61–64%, and 86% gives 63–66%. A 62–64% revenue-share print therefore needs about 85–86% volume share, but share has risen only about 1 point a quarter lately (82→83%). The guide-consistent print (~68–69% revenue share) needs ~88% volume share. Break signals proposed: derived 6000 revenue above $210M (vs $219M in Q2); X pull-through below $1.20M; X revenue share below 60%; clinical growth below 12%. Rounding leaves r known only to ±0.03, so no single-quarter move in r is informative. | OPINION (calc) | File 52 key finding 10 and §4b | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 49, 50, 51, 52)

Top-down clinical HT Gb is about 81–139 Pb per quarter, at an assumed net X price of $3.00–$1.75/Gb, so the named labs cover roughly 7–13%. Either E082's intensities are too low for the big oncology names (Guardant alone is ~6.2 Pb at an assumed 60 Gb/test) or most clinical Gb sits with unnamed labs. CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): E083 : Not comparable with E205. It is a forward 2026–28 forecast while E205 is trailing. Its 2026 base (+23%) is already overtaken by H1'26 prints (about +31% on its own framework, +33–34% with Gb weights). It applies revenue weights to Gb. It also implies total HT Gb of only about +19–22%, against management's '>30%' (file 52 §2a, §2c).
CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): E205 : Method B's +60–65% is not consistent with total '>30%' unless total HT Gb is ~37–39%. A defensible trailing range is +35–50%, central about +42%, and the internally consistent method is the one that anchors on total +30–38% (file 52 §2b). The ledger's 'bracketed +35–60%' (correction 246) should be narrowed.
CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): E008 : The single 'residual −3.7%' hides opposite-signed platform moves. At total Gb +30% the 6000 is +7% $/Gb and the X is −10%. It is a blend, not a within-platform price change (file 52 §2d).
CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): E184 / E176 / E159 : The 'Q3'26 is a 14-week quarter, about +7% to Q3 growth' note is wrong. Q3 is 13 weeks, and the extra week is in Q4 (Q2'26 call l.328). Remove the +7% Q3 adjustment (file 52).
CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): E212 : 'No explicit minimum-purchase quantity' needs qualifying. The 2014 First Amendment (refiled with the Q2'26 10-Q) makes the 4th calendar month of each forecast binding, with ±25% variance. This is a short-horizon take-or-pay, not a dollar minimum. E227 separately notes a redacted quarterly minimum in the Eleventh Amendment (file 50 item 7).
CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): E252 : The Europe PMC abstract of the Ultima preprint (PPR1231843) now confirms the finding beyond snippet level. The UG100 'reduces genome sequencing costs to $80' but 'technical limitations in resolving long homopolymer regions undermine the application of this technology to short variant calling in clinical settings'. The comparator is the NovaSeq 6000. The abstract has no numbers, and the full text is still unread (file 51).
CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): E197 / E220 : Do NOT add the file-49 AIP table yet. The numbers do not reconcile. The reported revenue actual is $4,369M, against $4,343M in E220 (possibly an AIP-adjusted basis). Op income at 103% of target should pay at or above 100%, and revenue at 98% of target cannot produce the stated '83%' as a payout. Together these cannot produce the 77% funding already in E197. The source was a search summary of the proxy, so the thresholds, targets and actuals are suspect until the CD&A is read directly (file 49).
CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): E154 / E222 : The 30 Apr, 2 Apr and 12–13 Aug 8-Ks in file 49 add only trivia ($314M left on the 2024 authorization, a $1.0B revolver to Aug 2031, a 4.950% 2029 coupon, and the three-director retirement already in E222). None changes the management assessment, so do not add them.
CORRECTION (49, 50, 51, 52): Open Offer window (E233 / gap 3) : Agent 50 found the open-offer terms page gives a 30 Mar 2021 availability date and no expiry, whereas E233 infers about Aug 2027 from the Form 10 'six years from closing'. The Aug 2027 inference stays unverified. E273 | COMPETITORS | Illumina's own comparison (2025-10-07) says NovaSeq X makes '6x fewer SNV errors and 22x fewer indel errors' than the Ultima UG 100 on the full NIST v4.2.1 benchmark. It says Ultima's 'high-confidence region' excludes 4.2% of the genome, including 793 disease genes such as BRCA1 and FMR1. CORRECTION (53, 54, 55, 56): E257 : The GRAIL royalty is not a flat 'in perpetuity' payment. The 4th amendment (21 Jun 2024) suspends it 'until December 24, 2026 or any earlier change of control of GRAIL', with no retroactive effect. After that the rate is 9%, with anti-stacking down to a 7% floor. It drops to 5% once $1B of cumulative royalties has been paid, and the suspension period counts toward that $1B. Illumina royalty income therefore resumes in Q4'26, which is a possible tailwind to check against the 2027 guide. (GRAIL Q2'26 10-Q, acc. 0001628280-26-054307, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001699031/000162828026054307/gral-20260630.htm)
CORRECTION (53, 54, 55, 56): E128 : 'Hartwig adopted UG200' is not evidence of an Illumina switch. Hartwig has run the UG 100 since 2024, so this is an Ultima-to-Ultima upgrade. Do not count it as share loss. Hartwig also appears in E124 as an AXELIOS early user, so its sequencer mix is multi-vendor. (aijourn mirror of the Hartwig/Ultima release, 2026-06-12)
E274 | PRICING | Harvard Bauer Core raised NovaSeq X internal (account-code) lane prices from FY26 to FY27. The 25B-300 lane went from $2,059 to $2,426 (+17.8%). The 1.5B-300 lane went from $1,616 to $2,028 (+25.5%). The 10B-300 lane was nearly flat at $1,304 to $1,314 (+0.8%). The PO (external) 25B-300 rate went from $2,883 to $3,396 (+17.8%). This is one core's rate card, not Illumina's list. It runs against the falling-$/Gb pattern in E051-E054 and fits E064 (cost pass-through). It could also be the core's own cost recovery. The pages were seen only as search snippets (the fetch returned 403) and are undated. | FACT (snippet-level) | Harvard Bauer Core FY26 and FY27 NGS pages, https://bauercore.fas.harvard.edu/fy26-ngs-sequencing and /fy27-ngs-sequencing, seen 2026-10-11 (file 59) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 57, 58, 59, 60)

The high-single-digit remark may refer to high-throughput consumables only. Check the Q3'24 call before relying on either number (file 60).
CORRECTION (57, 58, 59, 60): E258 / E212 : Natera Q2'26 10-Q Ex. 10.1 is the original 'Supply Agreement (TG Consumables)' dated 16 Aug 2013, 'as amended September 18, 2014'. The First Amendment is not a separate exhibit. The refiled chain is therefore the 2013 base agreement plus the 2014-2016 amendments. The Third Amendment's ten-year term (about June 2026) fits the refiling quarter, but that is an inference and the reason is still unstated (file 58, WebFetch summary of the exhibits).
CORRECTION (57, 58, 59, 60): Wave file 59 $/Gb ladder : do not use it. It assumes 750 Gb per 25B lane (2.5B reads x 300), which conflicts with Rush's stated 930 Gb (E259) and Novogene's 1,000 Gb (E259). It also conflicts with E054, where UConn $2.94 per Gb implies about 1,000 Gb. At 750 Gb, UConn's $2,933.99 gives $3.91/Gb, which makes the file's 25B figures about 25-35% too high. Keep the dollar-per-lane facts (Harvard, UCLA) and drop the derived $/Gb until Gb per lane is confirmed.
CORRECTION (57, 58, 59, 60): E261 / E234 : file 59 re-confirms the HT-mode roadmap text and the GSA 25B-300 price of $24,155.04. This is corroboration only (corroboration +1 each), and neither source gives a price or date. Its note that a $16,000 'list' price exists is an unchecked search snippet, so ignore it.

CORRECTION (61, 62, 63, 64): E150/E151 and CORRECTION 21-24 : Meister/Corvex Illumina sales are ~3,059,258 shares and ~$592M, not ~3.02M sh/~$586M. The 19-21 Aug Form 4 (acc. 0001193125-26-361476) shows 679,574 sh (95,000 + 285,238 + 299,336; ~$145.6M), not 641,814, a 37,760 understatement. Adding 6,780 direct shares explains the 44,540 'gap', so that gap is closed. Window totals: 13-14 May 329,460 (~$47.9M); 28 May-1 Jun 669,596 (~$108.2M); 4-5 Aug 741,127 (~$148.2M); 19-21 Aug 679,574; 25-27 Aug 375,941 (~$85.2M, not 375,541); 2-4 Sep 263,560 (~$57.2M). The holdings chain ties to 770,350 shares after 4 Sep (SEC Form 4s, filed 2026-08-21, 08-27 and 09-08).
CORRECTION (61, 62, 63, 64): E146 : SomaLogic terms are $350M upfront cash plus up to $75M of milestone payments to Standard BioTools plus royalty streams, covering all SomaLogic shares and two Sengenics entities (8-K Item 2.01, 2026-01-30, acc. 0001110803-26-000014). The '$418M+ total cash' framing is therefore roughly the upfront plus milestones plus other items. Sizing of the royalties is not disclosed.
CORRECTION (61, 62, 63, 64): E217 / E054 / E259 / E055 : Illumina's NovaSeq X specification page gives 25B output of 8-10.5 Tb at 2x150, 5.3-7 Tb at 2x100 and 2.6-3.5 Tb at 2x50, with 26-35B single-end reads. The output therefore does scale with cycle count, and E217's 'template artifact' suspicion is wrong. Applying these ranges to the Jan 2025 list prices gives $14,500 (200-cycle) / 5.3-7.0 Tb = $2.07-2.74/Gb (E217 used a point estimate of ~$2.90) and $11,700 (100-cycle) / 2.6-3.5 Tb = $3.34-4.50/Gb (E217 used $4.68). Illumina's '$2.00/Gb' is therefore closer to plausible than E217 implied, though the 300-cycle list is still not found (calc on FACT spec ranges; illumina.com/systems/sequencing-platforms/novaseq-x-plus/specifications.html, fetched 2026-10-11).
CORRECTION (61, 62, 63, 64): E259 / open gap 2 : 750 Gb per 25B lane is too low for a 300-cycle run. Dividing the 8-10.5 Tb spec by 8 lanes (the lane count is the agent's assumption) gives ~1.0-1.3 Tb per lane, which supports the 1,000 Gb used by UConn and Novogene and puts Rush's 930 Gb at the conservative end. Rush's $4.20/lane therefore implies $3.2-4.2/Gb, not a fixed $4.52. The same 25B lane ranges ~2x across cores (Harvard $2.06 internal, UConn $2.93, TGen $3.14, UCLA $3.51, Rush $4.20-4.52), so the core price is mostly a pricing-policy choice, not a Gb difference. Each of these needs matching to cycle count.
CORRECTION (61, 62, 63, 64): E150 / E-numbers citing acc. 0001110803-26-000160 as a Form 4 : that accession is the Q2'26 10-Q filed 2026-07-31, not a Form 4. The data.sec.gov submissions-JSON summaries mislabel form types (0001193125-26-383924 showed as 'FWP' but is a Form 4; -000180/-000181 showed as '10-Q'). Re-check any ledger insider-trade citation of -000160 against the EDGAR index page.

The CD&A (AIP weights, thresholds and actuals) is still unread, so the 77% funding and the 'revenue and non-GAAP operating income' versus 'operating margin' label remain unverified.

E287 | Q1-OVEREARNING | An openFDA PMA database check finds that FoundationOne CDx (P170019/S045) was approved 2024-04-25 for 'migration of the F1CDx from the HiSeq 4000 to NovaSeq 6000 configuration'. It was a 180-day supplement received 2023-05-19, about 11 months before approval. The later F1CDx supplements S046, S052, S054 and S060 are labeling only. No PMA supplement for Guardant360 CDx (P200010, through S029 on 2026-09-18), FoundationOne Liquid CDx (P190032), Tempus xT CDx (P210011) or Guardant Shield (P230009) names a NovaSeq X. Documented FDA-approved 6000-to-X migrations for these CDx products therefore number zero, and the one documented move was to the 6000 in 2024. Caveats: openFDA gives short approval statements, not the SSED PDFs, and change-control plans or pending supplements would not show. This is a database check, which the E250 absence inference lacked. It supports the sticky regulated-tail view. | FACT | openFDA api.fda.gov/device/pma.json, pulled 2026-10-11 (F67) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 65, 66, 67, 68)

E289 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Providence/ISB Geno4ME clinical WGS assay (Wagner et al., Frontiers in Molecular Biosciences, received 2025-07-18, accepted 2025-09-16, PMC12554564) was validated on the NovaSeq 6000, with 24 libraries per S4 flow cell. Target depth was 30X (median 35.2-41.9X), with 100% sensitivity and specificity versus orthogonal methods, and more than 2,000 patients sequenced. That is roughly 125 Gb per sample (calc, ~3,000 Gb per S4). A new large-system clinical WGS program was still being validated on the 6000 in mid-2025. The paper gives no cost per genome and no parallel-instrument statement. It is one lab and one design. | FACT | PMC12554564 (F67) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 65, 66, 67, 68)
E290 | PRICING | University of Minnesota Genomics Center (UMGC) says AVITI sequencing is '33% less expensive per million reads (or per GB) compared to fiscal year 2025' and that costs are down 62-71% since FY24. The 150- and 300-cycle kit output rose 50% in the 15 Sep 2025 upgrade. AVITI24 300-cycle lanes cost $1,002-1,503 (750M reads) and $1,175-1,617 (1.5B reads). That is about $2.6-3.6/Gb for the high-output lane (calc, assumes read pairs at 2x150), the same range as 25B core rates (E054). UMGC does not own an X Plus and says outsourcing it is 'faster and less expensive'. This is a core-facility claim with no price quoted for the X. E296 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Caris MI Cancer Seek (PMA P240010, approved 2024-11-05, about 7 months after the 2024-04-01 filing) is named in the SSED as run on the 'Illumina NovaSeq 6000'. Run validity needs '>16 billion reads passing filters' and Q30, with '>100x' depth on key genes. It covers 228 genes plus MSI, TMB and a copy-number gene. It has 10 supplements through S010 (2026-07-02), and none names a sequencer. Caris is the largest WES/WTS clinical customer (E092), so its flagship FDA-approved test is anchored to the 6000. The LDT volume may run elsewhere (unchecked). This extends E287/E250 to a customer they omitted | FACT | openFDA api.fda.gov/device/pma.json P240010, pulled 2026-10-11; Discoveries in Health Policy 2024-12-30; CLP Magazine | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 69, 70, 71, 72)
E297 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Guardant360 Liquid CDx is a new PMA (P250027): received 2025-07-31, amended 2026-02-17, approved 2026-05-19 (about 9.7 months). It covers 741 genes plus 2 copy-number genes and carries over the 7 CDx indications of P200010. The SSED was unreadable, so it is not known whether it names the 6000 or the X. That decides whether E250 and E288 stand. If it names the X, an FDA pathway to the X exists. If it names the 6000, the May 2026 X move (E043) applies only to the LDT. The approval landed in the same month as the LDT move to the X. Guardant expects ADLT designation in 1H27. E287's 'no matching supplement' check missed this because the content moved to a new PMA, not a supplement | FACT (approval dates); platform UNKNOWN | openFDA P250027, pulled 2026-10-11; Guardant Q2'26 call 2026-08-07 (fool.com) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 69, 70, 71, 72)
E298 | Q1-OVEREARNING | FDA clocks for platform and content changes, from openFDA. Caris initial PMA took about 7 months; Guardant Liquid CDx about 9.7 months; TSO Comprehensive (P230011) 17 months initially (2023-03-20 to 2024-08-21), with supplements S002 (MSI) about 17 months and S006 (thyroid RET) about 9 months. TSO Comprehensive remains NextSeq 550Dx only through S008 (2026-09-04), so Illumina's own FDA-approved CGP kit has no high-throughput instrument after more than 2 years. F1CDx moved HiSeq 4000 to NovaSeq 6000 only in April 2024, about 5.5 years after the NovaSeq 6000 launched (Jan 2018). An approved-assay platform move takes about 11-17 months, and the lag after a platform launch is years. This quantifies E031's 12-18 month revalidation point and supports a long regulated tail | FACT + calc | openFDA PMA P230011, P170019/S045, P240010, P250027, pulled 2026-10-11; FDA recently-approved-devices page | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 69, 70, 71, 72)
E299 | COMPETITORS | First comparable-depth oncology WGS cost gap vs the X. Modelled tumor/normal WGS (90x/30x) cost is about 29% lower on AXELIOS than on NovaSeq X 25B. The cost is a model, not invoices, and sample counts and runtime were not disclosed. It is a research partner, not a CLIA lab. CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E287 : It says zero X-naming supplements for the CDx products checked. That omitted Guardant360 Liquid CDx (new PMA P250027, approved 2026-05-19) and Caris MI Cancer Seek (P240010, NovaSeq 6000). The 'no X pathway' inference is incomplete until the P250027 SSED is read (openFDA, file 72).
CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E250 : Still unproven. The Guardant Liquid CDx SSED names the platform that decides it. TSO Comprehensive is confirmed NextSeq 550Dx only through S008 on 2026-09-04. The TruSight Whole Genome IVDR page title mentions NovaSeq X ordering, but search results point to the NovaSeq 6000Dx (file 72).
CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E288 : The claim that the LDT captured the X saving while the regulated version shows no change must be qualified. The regulated Guardant360 content moved to a new PMA (P250027) approved the same month as the LDT's X move. Which sequencer P250027 names is unknown (file 72).
CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E043 : Add that the Q2'26 call transcript quotes 'completed the Guardant360 Liquid transition to NovaSeq X in May'. The relation to the new Liquid CDx PMA is unresolved (file 72).
CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E257 : Still rests on a search snippet. Ex. 10.6 as fetched was truncated at §9.1(c), so §13 (term and renewal) was not confirmed. The Fourth Amendment (Ex. 10.4, 8-K 2024-06-21) does not change term or renewal (file 70).
CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E092 : Add Caris per-case cost (about $1,425 vs about $1,340 a year earlier, calc) and FY25 gross margin of 66% vs 43%. Caris margin expansion is price/mix, not cost deflation (file 70).
CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E089 : Add Natera cost of product revenues per test of about $255 vs about $234 (+9%) against revenue per test +12.5%. Natera is not showing per-test cost deflation (file 70).
CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E234 / gap 2 part-number list : 20104704 is the 1.5B 200-cycle kit and 20085594 is the 10B 300-cycle kit. The 25B 300-cycle kit is 20104706, the 100- and 200-cycle kits are 20125967 and 20125968, and the 5B kits are 20145965/66/67. No 14B or 35B kit is listed yet (illumina.com NovaSeq X reagent kits page, file 71).
CORRECTION (69, 70, 71, 72): E060 : A $/Gb for AXELIOS from '$0.06/M reads' needs an assumed read length (file 71 used a 175 bp insert, about $0.34/Gb). That is an unverified assumption, so do not use it as a verified $/Gb until Roche's read definition is confirmed.
E301 | MANAGEMENT | GRAIL CVR and SomaLogic bridge: the $32M favorable fair-value change in SG&A (six months to 28 Jun 2026) covers both items. Contingent consideration roll-forward: opening $54M + $81M added (SomaLogic) − $32M fair-value change − $26M cash paid = $77M ending. That $77M is CVR $40M + SomaLogic $37M. The CVR share of the gain is about $14M ($54M to $40M) and SomaLogic about $18M (calc). The CVR pays 2.5% of GRAIL revenue up to $1B/yr and 9% above $1B, through Aug 2033, valued by Monte Carlo. The SomaLogic write-down reflects Q2 early-buyout discussions on the 2026 milestone and royalty obligations. The gains are booked in SG&A, so they lift GAAP operating margin; whether non-GAAP excludes them is not confirmed. | FACT (R43 roll-forward, R10 narrative) + OPINION (split is inferred) | Illumina Q2'26 10-Q, acc. 0001110803-26-000160, filed 2026-07-31, R43 and R10, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1110803/000111080326000160/ | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 73, 74, 75, 76)
E302 | MANAGEMENT | SomaLogic contingent liability was $37M at 28 Jun 2026 and Illumina paid $30M on 27 Jul 2026 to settle. If $30M was the full settlement, a favorable true-up of about $7M should land in Q3'26 SG&A. This is a small extra non-operating tailwind for Q3 margin quality, on top of the government-incentive gains in E147. | OPINION (inference from FACT balances) | Illumina Q2'26 10-Q R10 (acc. 0001110803-26-000160), file 73_76, 2026-10-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 73, 74, 75, 76)
E303 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Cohort inconsistency: E026 has year-1 revenue for the 40 converted clinical customers at flat to <+5%. At a 60–70% per-Gb price cut, flat revenue needs the converting Gb to rise about 2.5x to 3.3x (+150% to +233%), against company-wide Gb growth of >30% and uplift cases of 0–40%. The cohort can therefore only be mostly incremental volume (new assays, exome to genome), not like-for-like workloads moving over. It is evidence about expansion customers, not about the sticky 6000 tail. Break-even uplift on the full conversion is +150% at −60%, +186% at −65% and +233% at −70%. Even +40% uplift recovers only 5–7 index points (~$110–150M/yr). | OPINION (calc on E004, E026, E030) | file 76_79 internal framework, 2026-10-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 73, 74, 75, 76)
E304 | Q2-VOLUME | Clinical Gb implied by reported revenue: ex-China clinical was +15% in Q2'26 and about +17% in H1'26. At a −15% price decline that implies clinical Gb of about +35% to +38% (1.15/0.85 and 1.17/0.85), consistent with 'well above 30%'. The mid-teens price decline is therefore not obviously too low. The competing reading is a smaller clinical price decline, as in the JPM survey (E067, −4–5%). This narrows the E075 reconciliation puzzle to two cases that can be tested. | OPINION (calc) | file 76_79, 2026-10-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 73, 74, 75, 76)
E305 | PRICING | 35B stacking sensitivity: if a ~20% 35B cut stacks on a −15% base, the effective price decline is −32%, and clinical Gb must grow about +47% to hold revenue flat. With only a half-weight 35B cut (25B-flow-cell cohorts only, E028) it is +30.7%; on a −10% base with the full cut it is +38.9%. For +10% clinical revenue growth in FY27, Gb must grow +29% with no 35B cut and about +62% with a full cut on a −15% base. This extends E084, which only treated the 35B cut in isolation. The open question is whether management's mid-teens price model already includes the 35B cut. | OPINION (calc) | file 76_79, 2026-10-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 73, 74, 75, 76)
E306 | Q2-VOLUME | Two-year-stack arithmetic: ex-China clinical stacks are 28 pts in Q1'26 (20+8) and 25 pts in Q2'26 (15+10). A constant stack implies Q3'26 clinical of +13% to +16% because the Q3'25 comp (+12%) is easy, but only about +5% to +8% in Q4'26 against a +20% Q4'25 comp. A mid-teens FY26 guide with H1 at ~17% therefore needs Q4 well above what the stack implies, so Q3 tests the FY guide rather than the run-rate. | OPINION (calc on E073/E074) | file 76_79, 2026-10-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 73, 74, 75, 76)
CORRECTION (73, 74, 75, 76): E148 : the $32M favorable fair-value change is not a CVR-only item. It is the sum of the GRAIL CVR (about $14M) and SomaLogic contingent consideration (about $18M) over six months, per the R43 roll-forward. The CVR/SomaLogic split is inferred, not stated (Q2'26 10-Q R43 and R10, acc. 0001110803-26-000160).
CORRECTION (73, 74, 75, 76): E282 : reconciled. The $54M to $40M CVR move ($14M) does not conflict with the $32M gain, because the $32M also includes SomaLogic. Roll-forward: 54 + 81 − 32 − 26 = 77, and 77 = 40 CVR + 37 SomaLogic (Q2'26 10-Q R43 and R10). Gap 8 is closed.
CORRECTION (73, 74, 75, 76): E250/E288 : still unresolved. The FDA PMA page for P250027 (Guardant360 Liquid CDx, approved 19 May 2026) lists one supplement, S001, and names no sequencer, so E287's 'no X pathway' inference stays open. This is a fetch-tool summary, not a verbatim read of the SSED.
E307 | PRICING | AXELIOS simplex price converts to roughly $0.12–0.34/Gb, depending on read length. Roche prices simplex at $0.06 per million reads. At a 175 bp average (40B reads per 4-hour run) that is about $0.34/Gb. At 500 bp (10B reads per run) it is about $0.12/Gb. Cost per run is about $2,400. Illumina's 25B list of about $0.68 per million clusters (2x150, 0.3 Gb per million clusters) is about $2.3/Gb. That makes the AXELIOS simplex gap roughly 7x to 19x per Gb, not a clean '11x'. The comparison is not like-for-like (simplex vs paired cluster counting), and the Gb figures are the agent's calc. | OPINION (calc on FACT inputs) | aseqer.com/p/roche-axelios-pricing, 2026, fetched 2026-10-11 (file 80_83) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 77, 78, 79, 80)
E308 | PRICING | Dated 2026 core-facility rate cards show the in-family X ladder, a step not yet quantified in the ledger. On nominal yields (25B about 7.5 Tb, 10B about 3.0 Tb per flow cell): Duke (2026-02-23) 25B flow cell $22,574 internal / $25,686 external, about $3.01 / $3.42 per Gb. Duke 10B is $13,393 / $15,810, about $4.46 / $5.27 per Gb. Duke 1.5B is $4,038 / $4,851, about $8.97 / $10.78 per Gb. Missouri (undated, about 2025-26) 25B lane $2,529, about $2.70 per Gb; 10B lane $1,188, about $3.17 per Gb. Moving 10B to 25B cuts $/Gb by about 25–32% at Duke, Missouri and Utah. So further mix toward 25B flow cells lowers Illumina's $/Gb even with no list cut, and no core is near $2.00/Gb. These cards give the 10B/25B price step but no same-date 6000 pair. | FACT (prices) / OPINION (calc of $/Gb and step) | Duke medschool.duke.edu/node/8607 (2026-02-23); Utah HTG uofuhealth.utah.edu/huntsman/shared-resources/gcb/htg/pricing (Jan 2026); mugenomicscore.missouri.edu/ngs.html (undated); fetched 2026-10-11 (file 80_83) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 77, 78, 79, 80)

E349 | MCED | FDA panel review of Galleri, 23 Sep 2026: benefit-risk 7 yes, 2 no, 1 abstain; safety 10-0. Effectiveness 6-4 is already in E318. Reported PATHFINDER 2 results (303 cancers): sensitivity 35.0%, specificity 99.85%, PPV 77.0%, number needed to screen 202. Reported NHS-Galleri results (820 cancers): sensitivity 31.6%, specificity 99.74%, PPV 66.2%, NNS 196. Sensitivity by type: lung 63.8%, colorectal 53.3%, breast 19.1%, prostate 10.7%. List price is $949, with self-pay at $799 or less. GRAIL says the decision is still expected in early 2027. Sensitivity of about a third argues against a fast mass-screening Gb ramp. E359 | PRODUCT-LIFECYCLE | Illumina's product-retirement page (fetched 2026-10-11) lists only iSeq 100, MiniSeq and MiSeq RUO, with end-of-sale on 30 Sep 2025 and end-of-service-life on 31 Dec 2029. E362 | Q2-VOLUME/MCED | Scenario sizing of GRAIL's contribution to Illumina clinical consumables. Assumptions: per-test depth of 8.5 Gb or 34 Gb (both unverified) and $3/Gb (the agent's assumption). At 1M tests a year, Galleri is ~8.5M Gb or $25M at 8.5 Gb/test, and ~34M Gb or $102M at 34 Gb/test. Against FY25 clinical consumables of about $1.9B+ (>65% of $2,939M), that is about 1–5%. At 500K tests it is $13M–$51M; at 185K (FY25) it is $5M–$19M. Sequencing is only about $26–$102 of GRAIL's ~$382 screening cost per test. This is consistent with management's 'we don't really see that in the number today' (E078). Separately, the GRAIL royalty on net sales at the 7% rate GRAIL expects is about $49 per test (7% of ~$698 revenue per test), or about $9M a year on 185K tests. The royalty is not consumables revenue, and the 9% rate resumes after the suspension ends on 24 Dec 2026. | OPINION (scenario calc on FACT inputs) | GRAIL FY25 10-K (gral-20251231, search snippet); GRAIL Q2'26 release 2026-08-05; agent 101 arithmetic | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 101, 102, 103, 104)

E366 | Q1-OVEREARNING | Bounded back-solve of implied consumables gross margin, assuming a 40% instrument margin (30-50% range) and treating tariffs as cost of product. The unadjusted series fell from 78.8% (Q3'24, X at 35% of HT revenue) to 75.0% (Q2'26, X at 59%). Once tariffs are added back it is flat: 78.8% in Q3'24 vs 78.4% in Q3'25, and 76.6% in Q4'24 vs 76.8% in Q4'25. The tariff-adjusted correlation with X share is r of about -0.16 (n=6), against -0.88 unadjusted. So there is no measurable consumables margin erosion yet. This partly supports management's 'comparable at scale' claim and weakens any 'visible over-earning in gross margin' argument. Q1'26 and Q2'26 cannot be tariff-adjusted. | OPINION (calc) | F108 s2; 10-Q cost lines and call tariff bps | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 105, 106, 107, 108)

E376 | ANALOG-HISEQ | Sequencing consumables growth through the HiSeq-to-NovaSeq 6000 conversion: FY17 +15% (Q4'17 +31%), FY18 +23% (~$1.8B, with Q4'18 +16% after normalizing for stocking), FY19 +14%, FY20 -2% (~$2.0B, COVID). Guidance missed: 2019 was guided 'above 20%' and came in at 14%. FY20 was guided about +17% and came in at -2% (COVID-distorted). Q3'19 revenue guidance was cut to about 6% growth, citing population-genomics ramp, DTC and lower non-HT sales. FY17 comes from a snippet and the 2019 guide from a summary. | FACT | F107; ILMN Q4'17, Q4'18 and Q4'20 releases and calls; Q3'19 call 2019-10-25; 8-K a3q19earningsrelease.htm | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 105, 106, 107, 108)
E377 | ANALOG-HISEQ | Conversion took years, and a tail remained. Three years after the Jan 2017 launch, 'under half' of HiSeq/HiSeq X customers had started the NovaSeq transition (end-2019). The active HiSeq base was about 1,300 at end-2019, with about 600 inactive and about 200 decommissioned in 2019. HiSeq consumables fell below $100M a quarter in Q4'19. About 1,000 active HiSeqs and about 320 customers pending remained at end-2020 (small-model summary, unverified). At end-2018, about 75% of HiSeq customers had not taken a first NovaSeq. The NovaSeq base grew from about 285 (end-2017) to about 600 (end-2018). Illumina cut conversion cost for stragglers with v1.5 reagents, an analog for the 35B flow cell. This is the first quantified year-by-year tail evidence and it extends E181. | FACT/MGMT | F107; ILMN Q4'18 call 2019-01-30; Q4'19 call; Q4'20 call 2021-02-11 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 105, 106, 107, 108)
E378 | ANALOG-HISEQ | NovaSeq pull-through in the HiSeq analog was about $1.2M per system in 2019 (highest in Q4'19), guided $1.1-1.2M for 2020 and 'over $1.2M' in Q4'20. The 6000 earned about $670K per system in FY25 (E017) and X guides $1.35-1.40M. So per-system pull-through on the older platform roughly halved as the base aged and shifted to a low-use tail. Launch claim (Jan 2017): usage cost 20% below HiSeq X and 45% below HiSeq 4000. The large cut came later with S4. Clinical consumables were about $830M in 2019 (+20%) and about $890M in 2020 (+8%, Q4'20 above +20%). | FACT | F107; ILMN Q4'19 and Q4'20 calls; Bio-IT World 2017-01-10, https://www.bio-itworld.com/news/2017/01/10/illumina-launches-novaseq-sequencers-aiming-to-replace-1900-sequencers | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 105, 106, 107, 108)
E379 | CUSTOMERS | Personalis's clinical economics are reimbursement-limited, not sequencing-limited. Gross margin was 1.8% in Q1'26 (35% a year earlier) and about 21% in Q2'26, blamed on unreimbursed test cost. Q2'26 clinical volume was 10,384 tests (+199% y/y) and clinical revenue was $2.6M, about $250 per test (calc), vs a Signatera ASP of ~$1,275 (E089). Illumina is the 'sole supplier of sequencers and various associated reagents' with undisclosed purchase arrangements, and the Tempus co-commercialization term runs to 25 Nov 2029. A cheaper Gb would not fix this lab's margin. Separately, Fulgent said TruePath mean coverage rose from 40x to 60x to 70x (Q2'26 call), a small example of cost savings being reinvested in depth. | FACT/MGMT + calc | F105; Personalis Q1'26 call 2026-05-08 and FY25 10-K; Fulgent Q2'26 call 2026-07-30 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 105, 106, 107, 108)

On a like-for-like basis the rise is $564M to $629M (+$65M, +11.5%), or +$91M on a total basis. Reserve coverage fell from 19.1% to 15.8% of gross inventory (Q2'26 10-Q; F106)

CORRECTION (105, 106, 107, 108): E047 : The 'mid-teens price decline per year' model is lighter than the ~24% blended revenue-per-Gb decline derived for 2019 (50% data growth vs 14% consumables growth). That figure mixes platforms, so it is a rough check only (F107)

E418 | READ-THROUGH-MRD-COVERAGE | NCCN added ctDNA-MRD to the muscle-invasive bladder cancer guideline on 23 Jun 2026 (v2.2026), as a tool for risk stratification and for choosing adjuvant immunotherapy after cystectomy. It applies to patients with no prior checkpoint inhibitor. The named assay type is tumor-informed multiplex PCR-NGS (Signatera). FDA approved atezolizumab for ctDNA-positive MIBC in May 2026, with Signatera CDx as the companion diagnostic. This is the first NCCN-level link between a positive MRD result and a drug decision. The guideline favors a tumor-informed, single-vendor, targeted-panel assay, so it adds test volume but not necessarily Gb. Tumor-naive and WGS-based MRD are not endorsed. Category 1 is stated only by secondary and Natera sources. | FACT (secondary sources) | CancerNetwork 2026-06-23 https://www.cancernetwork.com/view/nccn-updates-bladder-cancer-guidelines-for-ctdna-mrd-testing ; OnCLive 2026-06-23; Natera release; file 125 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 125, 126, 127, 128)
E419 | READ-THROUGH-MRD-COVERAGE | NCCN colon/rectal ctDNA language (Feb 2025 update) is still prognostic only: it names ctDNA as a high-risk recurrence factor but says there is 'insufficient evidence to recommend routine use of ctDNA assays outside of a clinical trial' and advises against de-escalating care on ctDNA results. No 2026 colon upgrade was found. Colon is the largest MRD category, so an upgrade would be the main coverage catalyst for MRD test volume. This is a dated baseline, not a 2026 confirmation. | FACT | Natera/BusinessWire 2025-02-10 https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250210708213/en ; OncNursingNews 2025-02-22; file 125 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 125, 126, 127, 128)
E420 | COMPETITORS-POLICY | The BIOSECURE Act became law in the FY2026 NDAA (Dec 2025). It names no companies and relies on the DoD 1260H list, which includes BGI Group, BGI Genomics and MGI Tech. Complete Genomics is not on that list. The OMB list of designated companies is due by Dec 2026. FAR revisions follow within one year. Restrictions take effect 60 days (1260H entities) or 90 days (others) after that. Contracts signed before the effective date are grandfathered for 5 years. The Act restricts federal contracts and grants, not private clinical labs' purchases. Any BGI/MGI-driven switching in US clinical labs is therefore unlikely before 2027-28 (OPINION). | FACT (law) / OPINION (timing inference) | Arnold & Porter Dec 2025 https://www.arnoldporter.com/en/perspectives/advisories/2025/12/the-biosecure-act-becomes-law-in-the-united-states ; Mondaq https://www.mondaq.com/unitedstates/healthcare/1725294/the-biosecure-act-becomes-law-in-the-united-states ; file 126 | corroboration: 2 | (wave agents 125, 126, 127, 128)
E421 | COMPETITORS-PACBIO | PacBio Q2'26: revenue $39.0M. Consumables were $20.1M vs $18.9M a year earlier (+6%), so the +67% clinical shipments sit inside slow overall consumables growth. Non-GAAP gross margin was 36% vs 38%. Management attributed the decline to compute and memory costs and 'multi-system pricing concessions'. Vega placements were 26 vs 38. Revio placements were 20 vs 15, with 60% to new customers and 45% in multi-instrument orders. FY26 revenue guidance is $155-165M. This is evidence of price pressure at a challenger, not of share capture. | FACT/MGMT | PacBio press release 2026-08-05 https://www.pacb.com/press_releases/pacbio-announces-second-quarter-2026-financial-results/ ; file 126 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 125, 126, 127, 128)
E422 | COMPETITORS-LONGREAD | Radboud UMC NEJM paper, cited by PacBio, models HiFi long-read WGS as a first-tier rare-disease test. Diagnostic yield rises from 16.4% to 18.9%, which is more than 500 extra diagnoses a year. PacBio frames this as a first-tier substitute for short-read WGS, but its own 20x genome list price is $345. Genomics England awarded ONT a £5.23M long-read instrument and consumables contract on 6 Dec 2024, and has run more than 15,000 PromethION flow cells since 2019. That is small next to Illumina's £4.67M contract extension (E100), so this is a complement or pilot, not yet a volume threat. | FACT / OPINION | PacBio press release 2026-08-05 and Radboud blog https://www.pacb.com/blog/radboud-umc-research-highlights-promise-of-hifi-long-read-sequencing-to-match-standards-of-care-in-rare-disease-genomics/ ; UK Find a Tender https://www.find-tender.service.gov.uk/Notice/039504-2024 ; file 126 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 125, 126, 127, 128)

E427 | READ-THROUGH-MRD-VOLUME | IMvigor011 (about 761 post-cystectomy patients) drew Signatera every 6 weeks for up to 12 months, which implies up to about 8-9 draws per patient-year (52/6, derived). Results: median DFS 9.9 vs 4.8 months (HR 0.64) and OS 32.8 vs 21.1 months (HR 0.59). ctDNA-negative patients were 95% disease-free at 1 year and 88% at 2 years. Real-world cadence will probably be lower than the trial's. This is the first tests-per-patient-year anchor for the MRD volume driver and extends E418. | FACT (draw schedule, results) / OPINION (draws per year) | Natera release 2025-08-18, https://www.natera.com/company/news/imvigor011-bladder-cancer-trial-achieves-positive-results-with-signatera-strongly-predicting-adjuvant-immunotherapy-benefit/ ; Targeted Oncology 2025-08-18 | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 129, 130, 131, 132)
E428 | READ-THROUGH-MRD-VOLUME | GALAXY/CIRCULATE-Japan protocol: one preoperative draw, then draws every 3 months for 2 years, about 4 per year. The Natera ESMO 2023 update covers 2,625 patients. 24-month DFS was 88.3% vs 89.9% for ctDNA-negative patients with vs without adjuvant chemo (p=0.156). This gives a colorectal draw-cadence anchor, and the chemo comparison is not statistically significant. | FACT | ESMO Asia 2020 GALAXY TiP abstract, https://oncologypro.esmo.org/meeting-resources/esmo-asia-virtual-congress-2020/prospective-observational-study-monitoring-circulating-tumour-dna-in-resectable-colorectal-cancer-patients-undergoing-radical-surgery-galaxy-study ; Natera ESMO 2023 release (q4cdn PDF) | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 129, 130, 131, 132)
E429 | READ-THROUGH-MRD-COVERAGE | DYNAMIC-III (1,002 stage III colon patients, ESMO Oct 2025, Nature Medicine 2025-10-20): 3-year RFS was 87% for ctDNA-negative vs 49% for ctDNA-positive patients. De-escalation worked: oxaliplatin use fell from 88.6% to 34.8%. Escalation failed: HR 1.11 for RFS. The protocol is a single post-op draw at 5-6 weeks, so it is a low-draw driver. The escalation failure weakens the 'positive result leads to more therapy' pull and lowers the odds of an NCCN colon upgrade (E419). | FACT (results) / OPINION (inference) | Queen's CCTG release, https://www.ctg.queensu.ca/cctg_media_releases/dynamic-iii-trial-results-ctdna-blood-test-powerful-tool-guide-treatment ; ecancer 2025-10, https://ecancer.org/en/news/27168-esmo-2025-dna-shed-by-colon-cancers-may-help-personalise-postsurgical-treatment | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 129, 130, 131, 132)
E430 | READ-THROUGH-MRD-COVERAGE | CIRCULATE-Japan ALTAIR missed its primary endpoint (Nature Medicine 2026-06-08; NCC release 2026-06-12). In 243 patients who were ctDNA-positive after surgery with no imaging recurrence, DFS was 9.3 vs 5.6 months (HR 0.79, p=0.107, not significant). This is the most recent colorectal 'treat on ctDNA positivity' trial, and it was negative. Colorectal MRD growth therefore leans on prognosis and surveillance, not treatment-driven demand, which supports keeping the ledger's colon coverage baseline (E419). | FACT (result) / OPINION (inference) | National Cancer Center Japan, https://www.ncc.go.jp/jp/information/pr_release/2026/0612/index.html | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 129, 130, 131, 132)
E431 | READ-THROUGH-NIPT-GUIDELINE | ACMG Dec 2025 prenatal cfDNA statement recommends cfDNA as first-line screening for all pregnant patients (singleton and twin) for trisomies 21, 18 and 13, and says to offer sex-chromosome aneuploidy screening. This is stronger than ACOG's position that conventional screening stays first line for average-risk patients (PB 226, 2020; no 2024-26 ACOG or SMFM update found). ACOG, SMFM and ISPD still do not endorse routine microdeletion screening, and single-gene cfDNA is treated as investigational. An ACOG/SMFM move toward ACMG would expand average-risk NIPT tests, but at about 1-2 Gb per test (E082) the Gb effect is small. The ACMG page returned 403, so this rests on a search summary. | FACT (secondary) | ACMG, Prenatal-cfDNA-Screening-Final-122025, https://www.acmg.net/ACMG/UploadedPDFS/PDFDocuments/Prenatal-cfDNA-Screening-Final-122025.aspx ; Santoli 2025 overview, https://starlegacyfoundation.org/wp-content/uploads/Santoli-2025-An-overview-of-current-prenatal-genetic-screening-and-diagnosis-guidelines.pdf | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 129, 130, 131, 132)
E432 | Q2-VOLUME | Signatera is a bespoke assay of 16 tumor-specific variants sequenced at about 100,000x average depth, with an LoD of 0.01% VAF. The target-base arithmetic is about 16 amplicons x ~100 bp x 100,000x = ~0.16 Gb, so each plasma draw is well under 1 Gb. That supports E082's '<1 Gb' per draw. The one-off tumor exome dominates the Gb per patient-year (~15-25 Gb for serial draws, ~9-16 Gb for one draw), with draws contributing roughly 10-20%. Amplicon MRD test growth therefore lifts Gb far less than test growth, and only a shift to WGS-based MRD gives a large Gb step-up. | FACT (spec) / OPINION (arithmetic) | Natera white paper, https://www.natera.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Oncology-Clinical-Seeing-beyond-the-limit-Detect-residual-disease-and-assess-treatment-response-SGN_AV_WP.pdf | corroboration: 1 | (wave agents 129, 130, 131, 132)
